I can't make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the voices are a part of you or something separate from you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. Um... I guess they seem separate. Like I said, it sounds like it's coming from the speakers. I haven't really... thought about it like that.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices more likely to happen when you're under a lot of stress or pressure at work?\n\nPatient: I mean... maybe? I never heard the call-lights talking before recently. Or... or saw those hidden messages in the charts. \n\nIt wasn't until... um... until I got so wound up about the new rollout at work. I was just working nonstop, not really sleeping much. I just felt this... certainty, like I had to fix everything at the facility myself. So... yeah, I guess they only started after all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a hidden meaning or message in the patient records that the voices are telling you about?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I mean... I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern. Management doesn't really want anyone to see it. \n\nMy supervisor... she just brushed it off. Said it was just late entries, or... coding mistakes. But I know what I saw. And... I can tell they're watching what I write in the system now. Just because I'm the one who caught it. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would agree that there's a hidden pattern in the wound-care charts if they looked at them?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they'd agree. I mean... I already told you what my supervisor said. She just brushed it off. But... I know what I saw. It's there. Even if they don't want to admit it... I know they're watching me now because I figured it out. I just... I don't really want to talk about it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actually watching you or trying to hide something from you outside of what the voices are telling you about the patient records?\n\nPatient: I... I already said that. The pattern is there in the charts. Management is hiding it. And... yes, I know they're monitoring what I put in the system now. They just happen. Like... I'll just be at work, and I hear them coming from the call-light speaker. Or the vents in the hallway. Just... telling me which patient records got changed. So... yeah, I guess they only started after all that.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that there is a hidden meaning or message in the patient records that the voices are telling you about?\n\nPatient: Well... yes. I mean... I know the wound-care charts are showing a pattern. Management doesn't really want anyone to see it. \n\nMy supervisor... she just brushed it off. Said it was just late entries, or... coding mistakes. But I know what I saw. And... I can tell they're watching what I write in the system now. Just because I'm the one who caught it. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would agree that there's a hidden pattern in the wound-care charts if they looked at them?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they'd agree. I mean... I already told you what my supervisor said. She just brushed it off. But... I know what I saw. It's there. Even if they don't want to admit it... I know they're watching me now because I figured it out. I just... I don't really want to talk about it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone is actually watching you or trying to hide something from you outside of what the voices are telling you about the patient records?\n\nPatient: I... I already said that. The pattern is there in the charts. Management is hiding it. And... yes, I know they're monitoring what I put in the system now. My supervisor tried to pass it off as coding mistakes, but... I know they're watching me because I'm the one who caught it. That's... that's all I want to say about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the pattern you see in the wound-care charts could be explained by something other than a hidden message or management hiding something?\n\nPatient: No. I... my supervisor already tried to tell me it was just late entries or coding errors. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
